Elizabeth Debicki Thanks the People Who Opened Doors for Her in Her ELLE Women in Hollywood Speech

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Elizabeth Debicki knows that no career is built alone. On Monday night, the actress accepted her honor at ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration, taking a moment to recognize the people who helped make her career possible.

After being introduced by Alexander Skarsgård, Debicki thanked her fellow honorees before reflecting on the support she has received throughout her journey.

“I really love to work. It is my great love,” she said. “And standing here tonight, I feel a really deep sense of gratitude for all the people—the small army of people—who have fought for me to be able to do my work.”

Debicki recalled how casting director Ronna Kress helped her secure an audition for Baz Luhrmann’s The Great Gatsby, which became her breakout film role. At the time, she was just 20 years old, had recently graduated from drama school in Australia, and had never done a screen test.

“Now, Baz, who is my fairy godfather, he fought very, very hard for me after I auditioned for him,” she explained. “But the only reason I was able to get that audition was because Ronna Kress watched the shoddy rookie tape I made back home and somehow managed to convince Baz to sit still long enough to watch it (which, if you know, you know is an actual miracle).”

She continued, “Ronna fought very, very hard for the studio to fly me out here to L.A.—I had never been here before—and to read for the part. And truly, that changed everything. That was really, really my door. And Ronna and Baz, they held that open for me.”

Debicki also praised the other women being recognized that evening: Julianne Moore, Penélope Cruz, Florence Pugh, Inde Navarette, Rosalind Eleazar, and Carrie Coon.

“In your work, your audiences are so privileged to witness your talent and your intelligence,” she said. “And this generosity in the way that you work, it makes us feel sane, and it makes us feel seen. So thank you, so, so much.”

Carrie Coon Speaks Out About Women’s Representation at ELLE’s Women in Hollywood Event

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Carrie Coon had a question for her fellow Women in Hollywood honorees: “Can we start a studio?”

On Monday night, the actress took the stage at ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration in Los Angeles, where she called for women in the entertainment industry to come together and create more opportunities for themselves.

Coon was introduced by her Gilded Age co-star Louisa Jacobson, who celebrated her recent career achievements.

“Carrie is a crusader, always fighting tirelessly for artistic integrity in a landscape that seems to value high gloss and profit over honest, nuanced storytelling,” Jacobson said. “And if you know Carrie, you know that she is completely allergic to superficiality.”

In her acceptance speech, Coon reflected on the importance of women’s stories, drawing inspiration from feminist literature and End Times Fascism, a book by Naomi Klein and Astra Taylor.

“Patriarchy, capitalism...these are hierarchical systems of domination that rely on fear,” she said. “And in this paradigm, women and girls, like our natural resources, are a disposable commodity—and as such, according to philosopher Kate Manne—our stories are interchangeable and of equal power. But of course, they’re not. That’s not true. And we’re not a monolith.”

Coon went on to discuss the performances of her fellow honorees, arguing that their work represented something extraordinary. “In these women, I see the fight for the living world. I see moral clarity. I see imagination. I see courage,” she said. “And these are, in fact, the antidotes to fascism. That’s how important this storytelling is.”

Toward the end of her speech, Coon questioned how women in Hollywood could create lasting change beyond events celebrating their individual achievements.

“How do we forge our collective safety and solidarity in an ethic of communalism?” she asked. “Okay, so this is the part of the speech where I don’t actually have the answer to that question, but I make the appeal that nights like these shouldn’t be one-off nights.”

She concluded with the aforementioned proposition for her peers: “And so I’m going to start with a really stupid question, but I’m not afraid to ask a stupid question: Can we start a studio?”

Watch Coon’s full speech above and read her Women in Hollywood cover story here.

9 Classics Where the Audiobook Version Is Better

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Audiobooks were technically born as far back as 1877, with Thomas Edison's invention of the phonograph. However, it wasn't until 1932 that the American Foundation for the Blind officially named them "Talking Books," introducing them as a medium for visually impaired people to enjoy literature as an alternative to Braille. Throughout the entire 20th century, and even well into the 21st, many classics have been turned into audiobooks so phenomenal that they're arguably even better than the original book.

Audiobooks and physical books are, of course, vastly different media that fulfill vastly different purposes, so comparing them can feel a bit like comparing apples to oranges. Still, sometimes, an audiobook is so good that it becomes entirely reasonable to say it provides a better experience than the original. These are audiobooks that anyone who enjoys classic literature should consider essential listening material. "Classic" is defined for the purposes of this list as any book that was originally published before the 21st century.

Folkloric vampires predate Bram Stoker's Dracula by hundreds of years. Inspired by such stories, as well as by Irish folklore and historical figures like Vlad the Impaler, Stoker wrote the 1897 classic that is nowadays widely remembered as the most foundational pillar of modern vampiric fiction. It's undoubtedly one of the best horror books of all time, which made the announcement of an original Audible audiobook-slash-audio-drama all the more worthy of skepticism.

In the end, however, Bram Stoker's Dracula ended up being one of the greatest horror audiobooks that readers have been treated to in years. With a stacked cast that includes the likes of Ella Purnell, Jonathan Bailey, and Aimee Lou Wood, the audiobook takes the classic epistolary novel and makes it feel even more immersive, cinematic, and well-paced. It's worth noting that it's an abridged audio drama rather than a full reading of the original novel, which is not ideal, but those interested in such a concept should love it.

Originally serialized between 1875 and 1877, Russian author Leo Tolstoy's Anna Karenina is widely regarded as one of the most foundational pieces of world literature in history, a must-read masterpiece whose daunting 1,000-page length shouldn't deter anyone from considering it essential reading. Since it was one of her favorite books of all time, Maggie Gyllenhaal agreed to narrate an Audible original audiobook that really does justice to one of the best epic books of all time.

Tolstoy's novel was such a monumental artistic achievement that the author was notoriously drained and exhausted by the whole experience by the time he was done, and reading such a vast book can itself also be a massive undertaking. With the raw immediacy and many emotional variations of Gyllenhaal's narration, listening to Anna Karenina can be a far more engaging and far less tedious experience than many may initially think. At over 30 hours long, it's definitely a time commitment, but oh so worth every minute.

Upon publication back in 1997, J. K. Rowling's Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone (published in the US as Harry Potter and the Sorcerer's Stone) was an immediate commercial and critical hit, kicking off one of the biggest multimedia franchises in history. The book's first audiobook version came out in 1999, and the performers varied by region. Whereas Jim Dale narrated the book in the US and Canada, it was Stephen Fry's voice that was heard in the British and international releases. If there is any one Harry Potter book that's better heard than read, it's Fry's performance of Philosopher's Stone.

As a whole, this is one of the most essential fantasy books to read before kicking the bucket, but there's nothing quite like hearing it through Fry's rich, velvety baritone voice. On the page, Philosopher's Stone is beautifully imaginative and cozy, but at times feels like an overly simple debut novel in terms of Rowling's prose. Fry elevates every word and makes the entire story feel like a wonderful fairy tale being told by the fire on a cold weekend night outdoors.

One of Stephen King's first novels was 1983's Pet Sematary, published before he had fully established and cemented himself as the true King of Horror. Fans had been begging for an audiobook for years, and once the project finally materialized, it was Michael C. Hall of Dexter fame who publisher Simon & Schuster picked as the right voice to narrate the story. The result is one of the best Stephen King audiobooks to date, and considering just how many great ones there are, that's saying a lot.

Pet Sematary was already absolutely legendary well before 2018, widely regarded by many—including King himself—as the author's most genuinely terrifying work. However, once the horror aspects really pick up steam after a relatively slow-burning first few pages, Hall makes it hit even harder. Getting an actor well-known for projects as death-related as Six Feet Under and Dexter to narrate a horror novel all about death and grief was not only brilliant meta-casting, but also hugely beneficial for the story as a whole.

Multiple books are almost always brought up when the endless debate of what "The Great American Novel" is gets brought up, but John Steinbeck's East of Eden is the most noteworthy. This family saga was likely key to Steinbeck being awarded the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1962, and it more than lives up to its reputation as one of the most perfect American books ever written. The first audiobook version of the classic ever recorded was narrated by respected character actor Richard Poe.

East of Eden was already widely recognized as one of the most perfectly written masterpieces of all time, and it's definitely a must-read, but there's something about the way Poe complements the narrative that makes his version arguably even more unforgettable. Steinbeck wrote East of Eden in such a sweeping, almost Biblical voice that it almost feels like the narration of a legend, and Poe's resonant yet deeply human voice allows that narration style to feel all the more intimate and personal.

The book that earned Harper Lee a Pulitzer Prize was 1960's To Kill a Mockingbird, one of the most essential and widely discussed American classics of the 20th century. Though certain elements of its narrative are undeniably dated by modern standards, its heart will forever be in the right place, with its critiques of racial prejudice and systemic injustice being as timely and hard-hitting today as they were back in the '60s. In 2014, legendary actress Sissy Spacek was tasked with narrating the audiobook version of the story.

The reasons why this is one of those great books that are even better as audiobooks are aplenty, and even though the Pulitzer-winning original remains absolutely foundational, Spacek brings a level of nuance and authenticity to the book that feels like a much-welcome sense of modernity. The actress' beautifully melodic Texan drawl makes the small-town Alabama setting feel all the more evocative and atmospheric, and the way she flawlessly captures Scout's personality as both a child and an adult is incredible.

Stephen King's 22nd book was 1986's It, still remembered by many as the author's best and most iconic work to date. At over 1,100 pages in length, it's an absolute brick of a horror novel, but its epic scope still works marvelously all these many years later. To narrate the 2016 audiobook, Simon & Schuster decided to go for an actor who already had a pre-existing tie to the Stephen King universe: Steven Weber, who played Jack Torrance in the 1997 three-part miniseries version of The Shining.

It was already one of those horror books where every chapter is perfect—something quite admirable for such a thick novel—but Weber took that literary doorstop and somehow turned it into an even bigger, more immersive 45-hour juggernaut of an audiobook. The actor gives each of the Losers a distinct voice both in their child and adult versions, and he goes all in on Pennywise without ever teetering too far into distracting camp. Perfectly paced and intense to the core, It is a must-listen-to audiobook through and through.

American poet, essayist, and civil rights activist Maya Angelou was a towering figure in modern American literature, completely reshaping how Black American identity was expressed in the written word. The first of her seven published memoirs, 1969's I Know Why the Caged Bird Sings, broke new ground and became one of the first major autobiographies by an African American woman to achieve global bestseller status. When an audiobook version was released in 2011, it was Angelou herself who narrated it.

Taking one of the best memoirs of all time and turning it into an audiobook worthy of its legacy was always going to be a tough task, so who better to achieve it than the author herself? By having Angelou narrate her own story with her unmistakably theatrical contralto voice, I Know Why the Caged Bird Sings feels even more honest and intimate than it already does on the page, balancing poignancy and humor to absolute perfection.

Well before he wrote what many still consider the greatest and most influential fantasy epic of the 20th century, J. R. R. Tolkien published the fantasy children's classic The Hobbit, almost inarguably the most perfect fantasy novel of the 1930s. There are multiple audiobook versions of The Hobbit available, but one reigns supreme as perhaps the audiobook that most elevates the experience of the original text: the 2020 version narrated by Andy Serkis.

It's easily one of the best audiobooks of all time, with Serkis not only delivering a much-expected powerhouse performance as Gollum, but also giving each of the other characters a distinct voice that makes them even more memorable than they are on paper. The Hobbit is also filled with songs and poems, and Serkis brings them to life in a way that reading the physical book simply can't match. In Serkis' voice, The Hobbit really does feel like the wonderful myth that it was always meant to be.

Brian Flores: Vikings plan to rotate players in order to deal with altitude in Mexico City

📅 2026-11-17  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The 49ers are spending the week in Colorado in order to acclimate to the altitude at which their Week 11 game in Mexico City will be played. The Vikings are not.

But the Vikings have a plan for ensuring that their players are ready for the conditions.

“It’s a tough place to play,” Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores told reporters on Tuesday regarding the game in Mexico. “I think it’s certainly taxing, you know, I think you’re — I think as a staff, we’re already talking about, ‘Hey, we’re gonna have to rotate these guys and get, you know, everybody’s gonna have to be ready to play.’ And so, yeah, I mean, it’s certainly something that we’re going to have to deal with.

“But there’s always, you know, elements that we’re dealing with, whether it’s cold or rain or things like that. Obviously, we’re indoors, so we don’t deal with much of that. But that’s the environment we’re gonna be in. I think we prepare them to play in that environment, but I’m not gonna be silly and say, ‘Hey, everybody, hey, you’ve got to go in there for 12 straight plays.’ I mean, we’re not doing that.

“So we’ll have rotations, we’ll have a plan in place. Inevitably, we’re gonna have to fight through some of that. It’s not gonna be your average game where, you know, altitude, you just have — you literally have less oxygen. So we’ve got to do a great job as a staff of making sure — watching guys, and making sure we got guys in and out, you know, as much as we can.”

His broader message to the defense is clear: “We’re gonna need everybody.”

Regardless of the plan to rotate players, the question becomes whether the 49ers will be able to rely more heavily on their best players because they’re properly acclimated to the thinner air. The Vikings are choosing a different strategy, and it’s going to create an obvious apples to apples comparison between the approaches that the two teams have taken — especially if the 49ers players seem to be fresh and the Vikings players seem to be gassed.

Gael Cameron Says Her ‘Below Deck’ Romance With Nathan Gallagher Was Abusive: ‘I Am Still Scared’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Almost a year after Below Deck star Nathan Gallagher was arrested for domestic abuse in Australia — and 12 weeks after the news went public — Gallagher’s former partner and cast member Gael Cameron is finally speaking out about their relationship.

Cameron broke her silence on an episode of the podcast We Need To Talk, hosted by Paul C. Brunson, where she publicly accused Gallagher of abuse for the first time and described a toxic relationship that’s left her in fear to this day.

“I know that he is not afraid to do anything to throw me under. He’s not afraid to lie… He’s always had this idea that he has ownership or control over me,” Cameron said tearfully. “I still feel that. And I am still scared of him. And that’s why I’ve not said anything, even though he’s thrown all these accusations out there.”

Gallagher and Cameron met while filming Season Nine of Below Deck: Mediterranean in 2023, both working on the superyacht’s deck team. Over three seasons, their relationship evolved from flirty co-workers to welcoming a son together last June. But this August — halfway through a season where Gallagher struggled to juggle his bosun duties and be a present father — news broke of Gallagher’s December 2025 arrest on domestic violence charges. According to Australian police, Gallagher attacked Cameron at her apartment in Sydney, resulting in his arrest on Dec. 27, 2025. During the physical altercation, Gallagher “placed both of his hands around the victim’s neck applying enough pressure which made her unable to breathe,” according to police records. He was charged with assault occasioning actual bodily harm, common assault, and intentionally choking a person without consent.

Although Gallagher previously told Rolling Stone he had proof that would clear his name, he failed to appear at a mandated court hearing in September. As a result, he was convicted by default on charges of assault. Australian authorities issued a warrant for his arrest, noting Gallagher was suspected to have fled the country. Bravo has since announced it has no plans to work with him.  Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Both Bravo and Gallagher declined to comment further to Rolling Stone.

During the podcast episode, Cameron charts the timeline of her relationship with Gallagher, including the behind-the-scenes struggles that kept her silent and afraid. Cameron first met Gallagher while filming Season Nine of Below Deck: Mediterranean in 2023. At the time, she was dating another man but began a friendship with Gallagher. Their working relationship was often “playfully flirty” when the cameras were rolling, but they didn’t start seeing each other until filming wrapped.

In 2023, the two were in a long-distance relationship, but Cameron said there were early signs of anger issues in Gallagher. When they were able to meet up, Cameron described his “love bombing,” showering her with gifts and affection. When she found out she was pregnant, Gallagher convinced her to keep the baby — the first of many moves which she claims he did in order to isolate her.

“If I ever said, ‘I don’t know if I can do this. I don’t think it’s the right time,’ He would make me feel very bad about myself. He would make me feel very guilty and just terrible about myself,” she said. “I have quite a conservative mum. I couldn’t talk to anyone. So he was kind of my only sounding board because I was so afraid to tell anyone.”

But the pregnancy only aggravated Gallagher’s behavior, according to Cameron. She said that she spent close to “90 percent” of her pregnancy in tears, often feeling like any small mistake would trigger Gallagher’s anger. In one instance, she describes that while drunk, Gallagher jumped out of a moving car rather than talk to her. Once a social worker told her the relationship was “unsafe,” Cameron began reevaluating if they could stay together long term. “I’d be told to shut the fuck up. I’d be called a bitch, a slut, and all these horrible things,” she says. “There were multiple times when I was like ‘No no no, this cannot continue,’ because my son cannot grow up thinking that this is how you speak to women.” But then Below Deck came to call.  Related Content Hayden Panettiere's Mother Blames Brian Hickerson For Star’s Death Kid Rock's Nashville Bar Sued After Bouncers Allegedly Beat Patron Who 'Tapped' Guitar Lawsuit Accusing Selena Gomez and Wondermind Co-Founders of Fraud Is Dismissed Priscilla Presley Scores Partial Win Over Fraud Suit, But Claims About Lisa Marie’s Death Can Stand

When Gallagher flew to Croatia to film Below Deck: Mediterranean Season 11, Cameron didn’t initially want to go. She appeared on the series as a guest star, and lived off of the boat with her infant son. But she believes Gallagher pushed her to come to Croatia as another form of control. “It felt as though he was very worried about who I spoke to or who I was around when he wasn’t around,” she says. “There was this element of control that he would lose over me if I wasn’t in the same country as him.” She also describes incidents during heated fights, when Gallagher would record on his phone, pretending that she was striking him or hitting him unprovoked. Things came to a head on Dec. 27, 2025, when Cameron said Gallagher choked her while their son was in her arms, which led to Gallagher’s arrest and subsequent conviction. Cameron appeared visibly emotional when she recounted the night.

“When I was in the bathroom, and he had his hands around my neck and attempted to lift, my son was in my arms. All I could think is, ‘If I fall, so does he.’ From that moment, I should have known there’s no return,” she says. “When he had me pinned down and he was striking me on the jaw, I actually sat there and I asked if he was going to kill me. There was a moment where I actually asked that question. The reason I say this is because if things had not gotten that bad, I would never have gotten out. That’s the scary thing.” Trending Stories Watch Buffalo Springfield Come Together for the First Time in Many Years Jennifer Lopez Teases New Music on ‘Fallon’: ‘This Does Feel Like a Happy Era’ Cynthia Erivo, Travis Barker Honor Phil Collins With Big Vocals, Big Drums at Rock Hall Ceremony Priscilla Presley Scores Partial Win Over Fraud Suit, But Claims About Lisa Marie’s Death Can Stand

According to Cameron, she has not spoken to Gallagher or been in contact since he fled the country. But she says that his departure has changed her life for the better.

“Him leaving was the biggest weight off my shoulders. All of a sudden, I could have my family over to help me look after my son when I went to work. I could breathe again,” she says. “I had been living this hell. So life felt so bright.”

Billy Bob Thornton's A Simple Plan Is Now Streaming Free On Tubi

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Katrina joined ScreenRant as a news writer in 2024, covering box office, expert interviews, exclusives, and new releases. Her work has appeared in CBR, All About Jazz, Rising Artists, and numerous other publications. She has an MFA in Creative Writing and has published more than 20 books. She currently writes for ScreenRant’s movie features team.

Billy Bob Thornton isn't a stranger to the thriller genre. His breakthrough came in 1992, when he co-wrote and starred in the critically acclaimed crime thriller One False Move. Since then, he has received three Academy Award nominations in his career. And now one of his best movies - A Simple Plan - is streaming for free.

He wrote and directed the 1996 thriller Sling Blade and also starred in the lead role, Karl Childers, which earned him his first Oscar nomination as an actor. His script won the Academy Award in the Best Adapted Screenplay category that year.

While Sling Blade may have been a career-defining project for Thornton, two years later, he starred in an acclaimed but lesser-known crime thriller that led to his third Oscar nomination.

Directed by Sam Raimi, A Simple Plan features Thornton as Jacob, the odd older brother of Hank Mitchel, who discovered 4.4 million dollars and a plane with their friend Lou. The movie follows the three as they decide to keep the money.

Adapted from Scott B. Smith's novel of the same name, A Simple Plan's premise is very straightforward, but the escalation comes as a surprise. The movie is brilliantly paced, with the first half loaded with psychological tension and suspense and the second half filled with action and consequences.

Thornton's character, Jacob, is layered and tragic. He first appeared to be somewhat goofy and greedy, fast to side with Lou who proposed on taking the 4.4 million dollars. His brother Hank appeared to be the more law-abiding person, who was against stealing. However, as the film progresses, viewers will soon find the roles reversed.

A Simple Plan received high praise from critics, including Roger Ebert, when it came out in 1998. In addition to Thornton's Best Supporting Actor nomination, Smith's script also received a nomination for Best Adapted Screenplay. It's hard to imagine another crime thriller matching the brilliance of Sling Blade, but A Simple Plan comes very close.

Billy Bob Thornton's performance is nuanced and deliberate. He makes Jacob seem like the least likable and least intelligent brother at first, but there are moments where the characters manage to surprise viewers. Jacob has depth and a conscience, which makes his tragic ending heartbreaking.

What's even more uncanny is how much the Landman actor seems to reveal about Jacob and Hank's father through his delivery of dialogue. He peels back the layers of an easily overlooked, normal-day guy and reveals the person underneath, who is good-natured, kind, observant, and almost too disillusioned and witty for his own good. Even though Jacob isn't the lead character, he manages to steal all the spotlight.

A Simple Plan officially joined Tubi's free streaming platform in November, in addition to Pluto TV, Paramount+, and YouTube subscriptions. Despite the 1998 neo-noir crime thriller being a masterpiece, it's often overlooked in both Sam Raimi and Billy Bob Thornton's filmographies.

Several factors have contributed to its lack of hype. Known for directing horror and action movies such as Evil Dead and the original Spider-Man trilogy, Raimi has a kinetic visual style. The film's slow-burning, atmospheric style takes a detour from the director's usual high-energy, flashy aesthetics.

It also came out two years after Fargo, which established a cultural touchstone by blending dark comedy with a snowbound Midwestern crime aesthetic. A Simple Plan is often considered a darker and bleaker cousin of the Coen brothers' film. While A Simple Plan was critically acclaimed, the movie wasn't a huge box office hit. It struggled in theaters and grossed only $16 million.

Google commemorates Robert Pattinsons James Bond casting with a sneaky Easter egg

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Robert Pattinson's casting as James Bond has left Google feeling shaken, not stirred.

Following the announcement that Pattinson would be our next 007, Google introduced a new Bond-themed Easter egg. If you search up Pattinson's name, the search results shake a little, and a small twist of lemon falls from the top of the screen to rest daintily on Pattinson's head.

The Easter egg is a nod to James Bond's classic cocktail order: a vodka martini, shaken, not stirred. Google has essentially turned Pattinson into the cocktail he'll be ordering onscreen.

Pattinson's casting as Bond came after weeks of speculation and reported screen tests for potential Bond candidates, including Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, Josh O'Connor, and Nicholas Hoult.

Pattinson will star in the as-yet unnamed 26th Bond film, the first since Daniel Craig took his last bow in 2021's No Time To Die. Dune helmer Denis Villeneuve is set to direct. The pair have also worked together on the upcoming Dune: Part Three. Villeneuve and Pattinson's Bond film does not yet have a release date.

Bond won't be the first franchise Pattinson has led to be commemorated with a Google Easter egg. In 2022, tied to The Batman's release, if you searched terms such as "Bruce Wayne" or "Gotham City," a yellow Bat-Signal would appear alongside the results. Clicking on it turned the page into a moody Gotham night, complete with thunder, the Bat-Signal, and Batman swinging around the screen.

Plus, if you search up "Twilight" now, you'll be treated to a small line of scrolling text that reads "hoa hoa hoa season," flanked by falling leaf emojis. The Easter egg references Twilight fans' loving nickname for fall, drawn from the so-called "hoa hoa hoa" vocals of Blue Foundation's "Eyes on Fire" from the Twilight soundtrack. Netflix's New Psychological Thriller Series With Near Perfect RT Audience Score Defines A Major Era

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix's new psychological thriller series that has become a global streaming sensation comes to cement a 2026 trend and define a major era for the streamer. Netflix has always had success when it comes to thrillers, which is no surprise, as the first Netflix Original series to make waves, both in terms of viewership and awards, was Kevin Spacey's House of Cards.

The political thriller series has since ended, but it went on a successful 6-season run, being a long-running hit for the streamer. Over the years, Netflix's hall of thriller series has been upgraded with a wide array of shows, including political, spy, legal, medical, and many other thriller subgenres, with all of those shows fitting Netflix's patented binge release model.

Penn Badgley's You, at five seasons, Money Heist, also at five seasons, The Diplomat, recently renewed for season 5, and others have gotten to go on long runs at the streamer. In fact, one of Netflix's Originals with the most seasons is the Spanish thriller Elite, at a whopping eight seasons. Now comes a new psychological genre series to shake things up on the streaming service.

The Body is Netflix's latest psychological thriller series, having released all of its eight episodes on the streamer on November 11. It has instantly become a global sensation, and the show taps into a pocket of the genre that has led to some of the most exciting 2026 Netflix releases, defining a major era for the streaming service in comparison to one of the years best Netflix shows so far.

The Body taps into the same supernatural psychological thriller territory of the Netflix show Something Very Bad Is Going to Happen, which was released on the streaming service back on March 26, also with eight episodes. The latter focused on Camila Morrone's Rachel, a bride who discovers that not only is she marrying into a creepy rich family when she goes to their winter home in the woods with her groom, but also has to deal with a dark curse that could take her life on the upcoming wedding day. Something Very Bad Is Going to Happen topped Netflix's charts.

According to data from FlixPatrol, Something Very Bad Is Going to Happen was the 47th most-watched TV show on Netflix for the first semester of 2026, racking up 20.3 million views. The Body now comes to take its place as the premier Netflix supernatural psychological thriller series of 2026. At the time of writing, the new show is the most popular on the streamer worldwide, according to FlixPatrol. On top of that, The Body has surpassed Something Very Bad Is Going to Happen with both critics and viewers alike. On Rotten Tomatoes, it boasts an 87% critics' score and a 97% audience score.

Both of those are higher than what Morrone's show achieved, as Something Very Bad Is Going to Happen stands at a high 84% critics' score, but only a decent 63% audience score. The Body's supernatural twist comes from how a group of teenaged schoolgirls perform a sacrifice ritual, as a joke, during Halloween to win their dance competition, which the Prince of Darkness seemingly hears, leading to prophetic visions and all hell breaking loose.

Talking to What’s On Netflix, creator Quinn Shephard confirmed there are plans for where the show could go next if Netflix were to renew the new psychological drama. According to the creative, "There are some Season 2 ideas percolating in my brain and in Gina’s [Gammell] brain." If that were to happen, it would cement another way in which The Body beats Something Very Bad Is Going to Happen to define Netflix's new era of genre shows, as that thriller was designed as a limited series, not having any concrete return plans right now. As such, The Body can rise above it.

Mulder’s Return In Ryan Coogler’s X-Files Reboot Gets Definitive Update From David Duchovny

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

David Duchovny is ready to start believing again for Ryan Coogler's The X-Files reboot.

Duchovny memorably co-led the cast of Chris Carter's original sci-fi mystery series as paranormal believer FBI agent Fox Mulder alongside Gillian Anderson as his skeptical partner Dana Scully, which earned him two of his four Emmy nominations and his first Golden Globe victory. After reprising the role for the sequel film I Want to Believe and two revival seasons, Duchovny and Anderson bid The X-Files farewell while Coogler, beginning in early 2023, began development on a reboot.

After wrapping production on its pilot in June 2026, Hulu officially greenlit Coogler's X-Files reboot, with he and showrunner Jennifer Yale currently in the writers room for the new series. On the heels of this, GQ sat down for an interview with Duchovny, during which the interviewer accompanied the star to a live screening with a Q&A for his 2023 drama Reverse the Curse.

One member in attendance asked Duchovny about the chances of returning for Mulder for Coogler's now-greenlit X-Files reboot, to which the four-time Emmy nominee had a definitive update that he is indeed in talks with the Sinners filmmaker to "come do something" for the new series, but said that "we haven't figured out what yet."

Joanna Gaines Shares a Sweet Moment at Her Family’s Waco Farmhouse as Her Youngest Helps Celebrate the Holiday Season a ‘Little Early’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Joanna Gaines and her youngest child, Crew, are busy decking the halls of the family’s beautiful Waco, TX, farmhouse.

While some people are staunchly against putting up a Christmas tree before Thanksgiving, the Magnolia co-founder has already brought some seasonal joy into her home.

In a Nov. 15 Instagram Post, Joanna, 48, shared a video of her 8-year-old son excitedly turning on the family’s tree—a stunning display of twinkling lights and metallic-hued baubles.

The "Fixer Upper" star acknowledged the potentially premature timing of the decor with a tongue-in-cheek caption, writing, “For those who like celebrating the season a little early…”

The audio the mom of five selected for the clip really drove the message home, as she selected a song called “Christmas in November,” with lyrics that say, “They say it’s a little early, I say it’s right on time.”

This particular corner of the HGTV family’s dwelling was also featured on the cover of the Winter 2026 issue of “Magnolia,” with Joanna donning a festive red gown as she poses in front of a selection of jars of holiday candy.

The tree makes an appearance on the cover, as well, shining alongside the statement-making picture-frame windows and rustic checkerboard flooring that make the Gaines home so recognizable.

Set on 40 acres, the property has plenty of room for a large family to celebrate together. The family purchased the home back in 2012, and at the time, it was a quaint two-bedroom abode. After countless improvements—which most recently included an extensive kitchen renovation—it's become a home Joanna has come to love, despite being unsure of it initially.

Even though it’s only mid-November, the holiday season has been on Joanna’s mind for quite a while already as she plans ahead for all Magnolia has in store this winter. In fact, she kicked things off with a Sept. 9 post, when she first brought out a variety of wreaths and garlands for a holiday photoshoot.

In a Nov. 10 editor’s note in Magnolia Journal, she wrote about her philosophy as she heads into another merry time of year.

“Every season holds a blend of the big and small, the milestones and the everyday, the photo-worth celebrations and the not-so-picture-perfect moments,” she wrote. “There’s something about the holidays that bridges the gap between these.”

She noted that as the years go by, she’s “come to realize” that the most important memories are the ones that might not be captured with a camera, and exist only in memory.

“Maybe there’s no physical evidence they happened at all, except for the fact that you were there—and you felt it,” Gaines continued. “It’s these softer moments that give us life and ground us in presence. The days that held no agenda, the mornings I took slow, the smiles you won’t see in my camera roll because I was simply bearing witness.”

But that’s not to say she’s not planning make a splash when it comes to home decor.

In a chat with USA Today, Joanna revealed that her approach to holiday decorating has gotten more over-the-top as the years go by.

“The older I get, the more free I feel to deck the halls,” she said. “I do think this is the one time of year where you can really go big if you want to—or keep things timeless and simple.”

Although she’s well on the way to turning her family’s corner of Waco into a winter wonderland, Joanna understands the importance of staying true to one’s own desires.

“There’s no right or wrong because the holidays really are so personal and sentimental,” she said. “It’s just a matter of what you love and how you want your home to feel for your family.”

As excited as Joanna seems to be for the most wonderful time of the year, she doesn’t appear to be blowing past any holidays too quickly. She also has big plans for turkey day, an occasion that she looks forward to for its delicious meals.

“Food really is the center of our holidays,” she told USA Today, remarking that she’s been honing her own traditional recipes since she first married husband Chip Gaines back in 2003. “Thanksgiving especially is the day I can taste all year long because our family really does make the same dishes year after year.”

Hannah Chambers is a senior celebrity and lifestyle reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2026. She has been writing entertainment and lifestyle content since 2016, with her work appearing in Cosmopolitan, BravoTV, NYLON, Bustle, Elite Daily, and The Zoe Report, among other publications. Hannah received a bachelor’s degree in journalism from The University of Maine. In her spare time, she loves to chat about pop culture and is a major fan of reality TV.

Hayden Panettiere’s Mother Blames Brian Hickerson For Star’s Death

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

While the entertainment world continues to mourn the death of Nashville and Heroes star Hayden Panettiere, her mother, Lesley Vogel, is placing blame squarely on Panettiere’s former boyfriend, Brian Hickerson.

On Monday, Vogel gave her first public interview since Panettiere’s Aug. 16 death, speaking with Inside Edition about the often estranged relationship she had with her daughter and her emotions following her death. “Sadly, she allowed Brian to reel her back in,” she said. “Hayden had clearly let Brian somehow take over many aspects of her life.” (Hickerson has denied any invovlement with Panettiere’s death and has not been charged with a crime.)

Panettiere was found in her Greenville, South Carolina home, after police responded to calls for an unresponsive woman. She was 36 years old. In a public statement provided by her representatives, her father Skip Panettiere called her an “incredible light and a force of nature who brought immeasurable love and joy to all who knew her — and to the millions who watched her onscreen.” In Sept., the Greenville County Coroner’s Office revealed her cause of death as the “toxic effects” of fentanyl and other drugs. Tests found traces of Xanax, the muscle relaxant Methocarbamol, and Quetiapine, a drug that is prescribed for schizophrenia, bipolar disorder, or depression, but the coroner said there were no signs of trauma or foul play.  On Oct. 28, the Greenville Police Department closed their investigation, ruling Panettiere’s death as “accidental.”

Throughout her time in the spotlight, Panettiere was open about her struggles with mental health; including postpartum depression following the birth of her daughter but also anxiety and substance abuse issues tied to her early brushes with fame. She also had a publicly contentious relationship with Hickerson, who remained her on-again-off-again boyfriend until the time of her death. In 2020, she requested a restraining order against him. And in her May memoir, This Is Me: A Reckoning, Panettiere described their relationship being fraught with emotional abuse and physical violence. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

At the time of her death, Panettiere had recently finished a stint in a rehab facility. And according to a police report obtained by NBC News, Hickerson and his brother Zach were both in the home at the time of the actresses’ death. (Brian Hickerson’s lawyer declined to comment on Vogel’s claims. Zach Hickerson did not immediately respond to Rolling Stone’s request for comment.)

When news of Panettiere’s death was first released, Vogel told NBC News that she had been extremely concerned about her daughter’s relationship with Hickerson. “This person in her life that we have been trying to get rid of for quite some time was with her at her death, and that was Brian Hickerson,” she said at the time. “There is a lot of history in the past that Brian has enabled Hayden many times, and this is why her father and I had been concerned for a long time about him.”

But in her most recent interview with Inside Edition, Vogel placed the blame firmly on Hickerson. When she was asked if Hickerson was responsible for Panettiere’s death, she responded, “Absolutely. There’s no doubt in my mind. And I think that he knows that.”  Trending Stories Watch Buffalo Springfield Come Together for the First Time in Many Years Jennifer Lopez Teases New Music on ‘Fallon’: ‘This Does Feel Like a Happy Era’ Cynthia Erivo, Travis Barker Honor Phil Collins With Big Vocals, Big Drums at Rock Hall Ceremony Priscilla Presley Scores Partial Win Over Fraud Suit, But Claims About Lisa Marie’s Death Can Stand

Vogel claims Panettiere’s treatment for substance abuse was “going well,” but blamed Hickerson for their estranged relationship. She also claimed that Hickerson sent “harassing emails” to her after Panettiere’s death, threatening “action” if she kept making statements.

During the interview, Vogel also dismissed rumors that she was not invited to Panettiere’s celebration of life, instead saying she chose not to attend to keep the peace. ”I knew that some of the people there wanted to believe that I’m the horrible mother because Hayden was angry and made statements,” she said. “I just didn’t want to rock the boat either. It was probably for the best.”

UK's Health Service Issues Warning Over GTA 6, Urges Players to Take Care of Their Hands

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 is so massive that even the UK's National Health Service — commonly known as the NHS — has gotten in on the act.

To be fair, the NHS raises a good point with regards to the open world game's imminent release. On Instagram, it's urging players to take regular breaks in order to avoid issues like repetitive strain injury — or RSI for short.

In a cruel twist, the official account's admin has actually had to undergo surgery to fix their problem, meaning that playing GTA 6 at launch is going to be difficult.

"Thoughts and prayers for your admin. Bit of surgery to remove a lump. Great NHS service, bad timing," the post reads.

"Planning on dedicating time to gaming later this week? Try and take regular breaks, obviously. And be mindful of RSI," it continues.

In this case, RSI is caused by overuse of certain muscles in the hands and fingers, brought about by repeated actions. It's a pretty common injury amongst gamers, and without wanting to scare you off completely, it can lead to lasting damage if you refuse to properly rest the impacted area.

The NHS has got a whole page on RSI if you want more information.

Sundance Institute Partners With Dior to Support Female Filmmakers Through New Initiatives

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ahead of the 2027 festival, the partners announced the creation of a new fellowship for mid-career storytellers, an award for bold new voices, a brunch to celebrate all of the above and an appearance by longtime Dior muse Natalie Portman.

In January, the Sundance Film Festival will present its first edition in a new home of Boulder, Colorado, and two months ahead of the anticipated launch, the team has revealed the first VIP guest being welcomed into the fold: Dior.

Parfums Christian Dior has officially partnered with Sundance Institute for the purpose of supporting female filmmakers and storytellers. In doing so, they’ve created the Miss Dior x Women at Sundance Institute Award as a way to recognize “a breakthrough cultural force whose bold vision is reshaping the filmmaking landscape.” The honor will be doled out to a talent on the rise as selected by a yet-to-be-announced curated committee and presented by longtime Dior ambassador Natalie Portman during January’s main event. Related Stories General News Steven Spielberg and Kate Capshaw's Hearthland Foundation Donates $3M to Sundance Institute (Exclusive) Movies Sundance Institute Taps Tracy Brady for Chief Marketing, Brand and Communications Role

Also under the pact, they’ve created the Women at Sundance Institute Miss Dior Momentum Fellowship. The fellowship, created for “mid-career storytellers who demonstrate artistic excellence and a strong commitment to independent film, will support six artists on their second or third feature film projects through year-round support with direct non-recoupable grants, mentorship, coaching, cohort gatherings, bespoke creative support, networking and a curated Sundance experience. They’ll also get the true Sundance experience beyond Boulder through access to Sundance Institute educational and peer communities via the Artist Accelerator Program, ELEVATE professional development sessions, Sundance Collab opportunities and the Sundance Institute alumni network.

But wait, there’s more: the Sundance and Dior initiative launches will be the toast of a new event set to take place during the 2027 film festival — the Women’s Celebration Brunch presented by Dior. That’s where Portman will present the prize in front of a gathering of film industry insiders, press, creators and other VIP guests.

“The stories we tell have the power to shape how we see ourselves and one another. This is why supporting women storytellers is so important, not only because their voices deserve to be heard, but because they bring perspectives that enrich our understanding of the world. When a belief in emotion, independence, and conviction meets a commitment to authentic storytelling, we create more than opportunities for artists; we help ensure that women continue to shape culture through the stories they choose to tell,” said Portman, who serves as the face of the Miss Dior fragrance. “For me, Miss Dior has always represented the courage to embrace your own voice with optimism, independence, and conviction. I’m proud to see this spirit come to life through the partnership with Sundance Institute, celebrating women whose creativity will continue to inspire future generations.”

Added Michelle Satter, founding senior director of artist programs for the Sundance Institute: “At Sundance Institute, we believe every woman deserves the freedom to tell her story in her own voice, on her own terms. This shared commitment comes to life through the inaugural Miss Dior x Women at Sundance Institute Award, honoring a filmmaker whose creative vision makes a meaningful impact, and our ongoing Momentum Fellowship, which supports women directing their second or third fiction or nonfiction feature. Together, we’re investing in the voices, visions and stories that will move our culture forward. We look forward to celebrating this partnership at the Women’s Celebration Brunch presented by Dior during our upcoming Sundance Film Festival in Boulder.”

Film Adaptation of ‘Saint Philomene’s Infirmary for Magical Creatures’ Set from Danny Daneau, Laura C. Lopez

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Daneau and Lopez will pen the screenplay for the family film project from Inevitable Studios and Give Not Take Media.

Saint Philomene’s Infirmary for Magical Creatures, the middle-grade fantasy novel from Bill Cotter, is getting the feature film treatment from writers Danny Daneau and Laura C. Lopez and producers Inevitable Studios and Give Not Take Media.

Rehab Entertainment, Black Bar Mitzvah and independent producer Sydney Foos are also behind the project, which is being planned as a four-quadrant family feature.

The story follows a twelve-year-old boy struggling with a new school and a chronic illness who finds a passageway to an enormous subterranean hospital built to care for ailing monsters and magical creatures. He is called upon to help combat a dark force that threatens the peaceful institution. Related Stories Movies Sundance Institute Partners With Dior to Support Female Filmmakers Through New Initiatives Movies 'Hershey' Review: Finn Wittrock Plays the American Chocolate Titan in a Sickly Sweet Biopic

Inevitable Studios is the production banner housed within the entertainment and disability-focused Inevitable Foundation. Co-founded by Richie Siegel and Marisa Torelli-Pedevska, the goal of Inevitable Studios is to back film and television that is told through a disability lens.

Give Not Take Media is a multimedia entertainment company owned by Caring Across Generations, a national organization focused on caregivers and care recipients, that develops entertainment projects that engage with the experiences of caregiving. Give Not Take Media was behind the recent festival standout Take Me Home, which screened at both Sundance and Tribeca.

Terissa Kelton and John W. Hyde‘s Rehab Entertainment recently produced Apple‘s Harriet the Spy and its current slate includes a live-action Disney relaunch of Flight of the Navigator.

Black Bar Mitzvah, headed by Jay Ellis and Aaron Bergman, is the production company behind Amazon’s Black Box.

Lopez and Daneau are repped by M88 and HJTH.

Jaafar Jackson Shares What Jackson Family Holiday Festivities Are Like

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jaafar Jackson shared how he celebrates the holidays with his fellow family members during an exclusive interview with E! News at the CCA's 9th Annual Celebration of Black Cinema & Television.

Jaafar Jackson will always remember having a good time around the holidays.

In fact, as the Michael star—son to Jermaine Jackson and Alejandra Genevieve Oaziaza—explained, his family finds more than one way to rock with one another during the festivities.

“It’s a lot of great music playing, there’s a lot of good food,” he exclusively told E! News at the CCA's 9th Annual Celebration of Black Cinema & Television Nov. 16. “We usually love playing some board games. Pictionary is one of our favorites. It can be loud at times, I would say, but it’s great, you know, it’s wonderful.”

Also wonderful, as Jaafar admitted, would be what’s on the table, too. “There’s always some peach cobbler, apple pie,” the 30-year-old noted. “And I usually go it first—to the dessert spread.”

Sweet treats and family gatherings aside, Jafaar—who moonwalked across theater screens earlier this year with his portrayal of his uncle Michael Jackson—is also gearing up to star in the film’s sequel. However, if you’re wondering about all the details to come, you are not alone.

“I haven’t started prepping just yet, it’s so early on—I can’t really say anything,” Jafaar shared. “It’s in the early works so we’ll see as time comes. And I don’t want to give anything away but it’s going to be a very special, incredible journey, I will say.”

But for now, Jafaar is enjoying what the man in the mirror has accomplished thus far with his role.

“It’s so much to take in,” he explained of the response. “I’ve been just overwhelmed with just so much joy and gratitude because it’s been a wonderful experience for me to see how much this film has connected with people.”

“It’s brought back that nostalgia feeling to a lot of people,” Jaafar reflected, “who grew up knowing Michael and experiencing his music and artistry. So, for me to be a part of that and play a hand in bringing forth his music to a new generation, I couldn’t be more grateful for that.”

For a deeper dive into everything the Jackson family has said about the Michael movie so far, keep reading…

As a producer on the film, Michael Jackson's eldest child "was on set every day," lead producer Graham King told The Hollywood Reporter at the film's April 20 Los Angeles premiere.

Prince, who attended premieres in L.A. and Berlin, has also touted his cousin Jaafar Jackson's performance, writing April 11 on Instagram that watching Jermaine Jackson's son "step into the role of his uncle Michael is incredibly special for the family. With his talent, dedication and spirit, he was the only right choice to portray Michael and bring his story to the big screen."

When Colman Domingo, who plays her late grandfather Joe Jackson, said that Prince and Paris were "very much in support" of Michael, Paris begged to differ.

“Don’t be telling people I was ‘helpful’ on the set of a movie I had 0% involvement in lol,” Michael's daughter wrote on her Instagram Story in August 2025. “That is so weird.”

Rather, she continued, "I read one of the first drafts of the script and gave my notes about what was dishonest / didn’t sit right with me and when they didn’t address it I moved on with my life. Not my monkeys not my circus. God bless and god speed.”

She crystalized her take the following month, saying in a series of Instagram videos, "They’re gonna make whatever they’re gonna make. The big reason why I haven’t said anything up until this point is because I know a lot of you guys are gonna be happy with it" because it "panders to a very specific section of my dad’s fandom that still lives in a fantasy."

Hollywood biopics are "sold to you as real," Paris said, but they're "sugar-coated."

Meaning, she alleged, "The narrative is being controlled. There’s a lot of inaccuracy and there’s a lot of full-blown lies. At the end of the day, that doesn’t really fly with me. I don’t really like dishonesty."

Paris also pushed back against accusations that she "hates her dad…because I'm not, like, calling the shots on set being a big-shot producer of a movie that's filled with just inaccuracies. That's not my truth…I just prefer honesty over sales and monetary gain."

As the most private of Michael's kids, Bigi hasn't commented on the film, but he attended the April 10 premiere in Berlin.

“To have my cousins there and my other family members, that meant everything,” Jaafar told E! News April 20. “Because it is a family story, and it all started in Gary, Indiana, so it meant the world to me.”

Michael's sister, the youngest of Joe and Katherine Jackson's 10 kids, asked to be removed from this narrative.

“I wish everybody was in the movie,” sister LaToya Jackson told Variety at the L.A. premiere. But Janet "was asked and she kindly declined so you have to respect her wishes.”

In response to a March Page Six report that Janet had issues with almost every scene in the film and drew Jermaine's ire during a family screening, La Toya told Entertainment Tonight, "There was absolutely no problem whatsoever, none whatsoever. Please believe it."

Director Antoine Fuqua said he has "so much respect and love for Janet," telling Variety she was "supportive of Jaafar, and that's what matters."

Jaafar was "absolutely fantastic," La Toya told E! News at the L.A. premiere. "He just embodied my brother and I'm so proud of him."

The fifth-eldest Jackson sibling considered it "very important" that her family collaborated on the movie, telling THR, "A lot of times people think they know the story and they read about things, but when the family’s involved, the family can say yay or nay."

Jaafar left Marlon "smiling inside at how great he is," the sixth-eldest Jackson sibling told E!, adding that he assured his nephew that his performance was "unbelievable."

And Marlon said he didn't spot any inaccuracies in the film, sharing, "When I first saw him on stage, when I went to the set the first time, they were shooting the [Jacksons' 1984] Victory Tour…And I said, 'Wow.' He looked just like Michael."

Jaafar's father, suffice it to say, was "extremely proud," telling E!, "He deserved the part, he worked so hard."

But before Jaafar took on the role, Jermaine explained to Fab TV at the Berlin premiere, he wanted his son to understand "what he was going up against," because those "are a tough set of shoes to fill."

Michael's nephew Taj Jackson told critics to beat it.

"Sorry, media, you don’t get to control the narrative anymore of who Michael Jackson truly was," the eldest son of late Jackson 5 member Tito Jackson wrote on X April 21, per Variety. "The public gets to watch this movie… they will decide for themselves. And you can’t handle that. Can’t wait until some critics have to eat crow. And yes, I will be that petty."

Tito's youngest son wrote on X April 22 that he was headed out to rewatch Michael, because he "wouldn't miss this opportunity to be masked in the electrifying energy that will be in that theater for nothing. My uncle deserves this, my cousin earned this and his loyal supporters are owed this. The world will be reminded or learn who MJ truly was and I can’t wait!"

Roadside Attractions Acquires U.S. Rights to Thriller ‘One Second After’ Based on William R. Forstchen Novel (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Roadside Attractions has acquired U.S. distribution rights to “One Second After,” Scott Rogers’ action thriller based on the 2009 novel by William R. Forstchen. The film is set for theatrical release on April 30, 2027.

“One Second After” marks Roger’s directorial debut. J. Michael Straczynski and Nicole Roewe penned the screenplay. Rob Pfaltzgraff, Nick Reid, Lana Link and Matt Kennedy serve as producers. Executive producers include Richard Masson, Sue Masson, Kenneth Horowitz, Barbara Horowitz, David Gore, Cecilia Gore and Forstchen. “One Second After” is an MPI Original Films production in association with Startling. Related Stories Skydance Movie Studio Shakeup: Kira Goldberg Departs Netflix to Run Paramount With Don Granger, Jeff Goldstein to Retire, WB Sets Leadership Indian Actress and Director Nandita Das, Syrian Star Taim Hassan to Be Honored by Doha Film Festival

According to an official logline, “One Second After” follows “former Army Colonel turned college professor John Matherson as a devastating electromagnetic pulse attack instantly disables the nation’s electrical grid, communications and transportation systems. With modern civilization brought to a standstill, Matherson must rally his neighbors to protect their families and community as dwindling resources, mounting desperation and violent outsiders threaten their survival.”

Josh Holloway stars alongside Hannah John-Kamen, Steven Ogg, Famke Janssen and Mary McDonnell.

“’One Second After’ takes a very real concern about the vulnerability of our infrastructure and brings it to life through the story of one family,” said Howard Cohen and Eric d’Arbleoff, Roadside Attractions co-presidents. “We live in a world where we take so much for granted, and the film asks what happens when those systems suddenly disappear. We love that the film can explore something so relevant while being a gripping, wildly entertaining ride.”

Roadside Attraction’s other upcoming films include “The Gallerist,” a crime comedy starring Natalie Portman, Jenna Ortega, Da’Vine Joy Randolph, Sterling K. Brown and Zach Galifianakis, and “Babies” starring Seth Rogen, Anna Kendrick, Issa Rae and Dan Stevens.

Jena Malone, Suzanna Son & Sofia Mortera Wrap On Indie ‘Kismet’ From Giovanna Molina

EXCLUSIVE: Jena Malone (The Boroughs) and Suzanna Son (Monster: The Ed Gein Story) have wrapped roles opposite newcomer Sofia Mortera in Kismet, an indie from Radiance Pictures and Distant Films that marks the feature debut of writer-director Giovanna Molina.

Set during the desolate off-season on Fire Island, Kismet follows a melancholic 13-year-old girl (Mortera) who, in the wake of her parents’ separation, claims an alien has taken over her body to save her from her human emotions.

Recently wrapping production, Kismet co-stars Elliot Villar and Riley Vinson, with Zuzanna Szadkowski, Matthew Sean Blumm, Marta Pozzan, Caitlin Hagney, David Staats and Lorena Ramos rounding out the cast. The film is produced by Molina, along with Zoey Pressey, Alexander Spenser Gould and Nuer Taqa. It’s cast by Betsy Fippinger, CSA. Molina told Deadline, “It felt incredibly special to bring this talented cast and crew this fall to Fire Island, where I spent much of my childhood. Jena Malone is a coming-of-age sci-fi icon, so it’s truly full circle to have her supporting a young exciting newcomer like Sofia Mortera in a film that nods to Donnie Darko through a female lens. Throughout our shoot, the island took on a mythical shape that mirrored the uncanny nature of the film: beached whales, nor’easter floods, roaming deer, and a double rainbow, to name a few. Filming against this surreal backdrop was a remarkable experience.” Watch on Deadline

A Golden Globe nominee, Malone has most recently been seen in the Netflix series The Boroughs, exec produced by The Duffer Brothers, as well as Jack Begert’s indie Junk, produced by Darren Aronofsky. Upcoming, she’ll be seen in the horror thriller Pendulum and Arkasha Stevenson’s untitled horror film for A24. She is represented by Entertainment 360, Gersh and Hirsch Wallerstein Hayum.

Son is best known for her breakout turn opposite Simon Rex in Sean Baker’s Red Rocket, for which she was nominated for the Gotham Award for Breakout Performer as well as the Independent Spirit Award for Best Supporting Female. She recently appeared in Monster: The Ed Gein Story and will also be seen coming up in A24’s mystery film from Stevenson. She is repped by Curate, CAA and Jackoway Austen Tyerman.

Mortera voices Young Priscilla in Netflix’s animated feature Steps and has appeared in the films Come Out and Play and The Spirit of Innocence. Additional voiceover credits include Netflix’s CoComelon Lane and Prime Video’s Agent Zeta. She is repped by DDO Artists Agency and Mills Kaplan Entertainment.

A Mexican-Italian-American filmmaker from Brooklyn, NY, Molina’s work explores stories of isolation, belief and surrealism within a female coming-of-age context. She has received grants and fellowships from Sundance, PBS, The Future of Film is Female and the Saul Zaentz Innovation Fund. Her film Quaker had its world premiere at the 75th Berlin Film Festival and won Best Narrative Short at Bend Film Festival. She holds an MFA in Directing from the UCLA School of Theater, Film and Television and a BA from Johns Hopkins University, with degrees in The Writing Seminars and Film & Media Studies. She is represented by Circle Management + Production.

Cornerback Nazeeh Johnson is trying to find a way back into the NFL.

According to multiple reports, Johnson visited with the Steelers on Tuesday.

Inde Navarrette Credits Actresses Who Gave Her ‘Permission to Be Free’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For ELLE Women in Hollywood honoree Inde Navarrette, playing Nikki in Obsession was about far more than landing her first leading role. In her acceptance speech at our annual event at the Four Seasons Hotel Los Angeles at Beverly Hills last night, the actress spoke about putting everything that she had into playing the character. “I needed to break through every glass ceiling either school, peers, society, or even I put on myself,” she said. “I needed to be connected with that rage that I used to find solace in. I needed to be reminded that my voice, whether soft or loud, holds weight and creates tension that I get to be in control of. I can play a girl who has an internal world—crazy!—whom is the lead of a film and whom is not the perfect victim. Nikki was my opportunity to show the world what I knew I could do and to that, I have to say thank you.”

She went on to thank Obsession’s writer and director Curry Barker, along with all actresses who inspired her early on (including Bryce Dallas Howard, who presented Navarrette with the award). “Thank you not only to Curry Barker for writing a role that allowed me that chance, but thank you to all of the women that took that chance before me,” she said. “Thank you to Jessica Lange for showing me and all of her work that there is beauty in madness and to just give it to the world. Thank you to Natalie Portman for Léon: The Professional. It was the first time I got to see a kid in a movie being more than a kid. She was me. Thank you to Sally Field and Steel Magnolias for daring to portray grief honestly and the hardships of motherhood gracefully. Thank you, Bryce Dallas Howard and The Help, for portraying how intersectional womanhood can divide us and that women who look different than ourselves are no different than ourselves. Thank you to Florence Pugh, Carrie Coon, Julianne Moore, Elizabeth Debicki, Samantha Morton, Rosalind Eleazar, Penélope Cruz. Thank you for daring to be wild. Thank you. Incredibly, you have all given me permission to be free. Let’s all shine a light in each other.”

Watch Navarrette’s full speech above and read her Women in Hollywood cover story here.

11 Climber Gift Ideas You Probably Haven’t Thought of Yet, According to Editors

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

(Photo: L to R: Anthony Walsh, Etsy Glass Carabiner Shop, Metolius, Yosemite Climbing Association, Kong)

If you’ve already taken a lap through our gift guide and still need more ideas, we’ve got you. All three of us editors here at Climbing have selected our top picks for gifts this year, from gear we’ve got our eyes on, to accessories in the climbing zeitgeist right now.

Some of these editor gifts picks make perfect mini gifts for stocking stuffers or for that climber partner who you want to thank for giving you the perfect soft catch this past year. Other picks make a bigger impact, from a super lightweight harness to a great gym bag. Most of our picks are great for a variety of climbers, whether it’s your new boyfriend who you hope to convert into a climber, or your mentor who’s been at it for eons.

We hope these gift ideas make your holiday shopping as painless as a splitter hand crack so you can spend more time training or climbing, and less time scrolling through endless product pages and catalogs.

Originally designed and hand-sewn by Nate Barton—the Missoula-based climber who ran the meme account @beta.sprayer until 2024—the Lolo Hoodie V1 ($169.99) is a functional midlayer for cold-weather climbing and the flagship product of the fledgling brand Lolo Hardware. Its large, zippered chest pocket was originally intended to help ice climbers warm up their hands between pitches.

Having pretty much lived in this hoodie for the past three winters, I’ve found the chest pocket far superior to any sort of pant pocket for storing my phone, gloves, and snacks. Plus, with my items against my chest, I never have to worry about my phone freezing or my car keys accidentally slipping out during a chimney. From Joshua Tree in January to Canyonlands in November, this is a comfy and durable hoodie for the coldest days and nights of climbing.

It’s 2026, folks: If you’re still using mined chalk instead of seawater-sourced chalk, this is your call to finally make the switch. Climbing’s feature on the hidden environmental impact of chalk showed how the vast majority of magnesium carbonate comes from mines in China, where magnesite factories poison the water and soil of nearby residents.

Luckily, there are plenty of sustainable brands that produce seawater-sourced chalk by using solar power to evaporate ocean brine from seawater. Here are some of our favorites:

Shop Midnight Lightning Sea Grip Liquid Chalk

For climbers with pierced ears, a pair of carabiner earrings is a customizable accessory that never gets old. Recently, I’ve been getting tons of compliments on my pair of silver, locker draw earrings (yes, they actually screw and unscrew!) that I bought at the Yosemite Climbing Museum in July. Those are only available at the museum, so if you’re not currently near Mariposa, California, here are a few other great places to buy carabiner earrings online:

After breaking a cross-loaded carabiner in a scary trad fall, I now always add some sort of tape or rubber band to keep cam carabiners in place. I wrote about my DIY solution a couple of years ago, but now I simply slide on Avant Climbing’s Flip-Stop Cam Carabiner Stabilizers ($22.50 for a 10-pack). The Flip-Stop makes for easy cam clipping when mega pumped. But unlike my rubber band hack, it also keeps the cam sling open for clipping a long draw into. Crucially, like a quickdraw’s dogbone, the Flip-Stop prevents carabiners from rotating onto the dangerously weak X-axis. Nothing ruins the holidays like a pair of broken ankles.

The new Arc’teryx Alpha SL Harness ($180) may weigh less than the gift wrap paper you’ll hide it in. At an absurdly light 78 grams (2.7oz), the Alpha SL is for those going ultralight in the mountains, whether that’s an alpine rock romp or a big ski traverse. This harness is also surprisingly comfortable to rappel in, thanks to its uber-stiff Aluula-composite construction. And it fits a range of body types thanks to its adjustable leg loops and five waistbelt sizes. Just don’t set it down unattended on a glacier—it’ll blow away in the slightest gust of wind.

The Patagonia Airshed Pro Pullover ($169) might have been built for mountain running, but I wear mine year-round on all sorts of objectives, from sunny rock climbs to cold north faces. The Airshed Pro is a pseudo-windlayer: decently breathable for high-output activities, yet stills blocks the wind and has a water-repellent coating. The result is a shirt equally at home skiing on a glacier or rambling up a gusty rock ridge.

A note on sizing: Since the Airshed Pro was originally designed for runners, the cut is quite slim. I size up to facilitate a wider range of motion.

I have been loving the Black Diamond Solution Hoody ($299) as a light fall belay jacket and even for cold rock climbing when I’m too chilly to shed my jacket. The Solution manages to be super lightweight, movement-friendly, and breathable, yet warm. This is largely because the specific PrimaLoft insulation used in this jacket is made with technology developed by NASA for astronauts. Here on earth, it also works great. It honestly does feel like being wrapped in warm air.

The pocket real estate is also generous, and it’s nice that the whole thing packs into the left hand pocket so you can easily stash it in your pack without a cam or crampon tearing into the insulation. I’m also looking forward to using this as a mid-layer below a shell for ice climbing this winter.

Unless you’ve been living under a rock (which many of our readers hopefully have), you’ve heard about the new film about the weed plane that crashed into a Yosemite lake in 1976. What followed was a tragicomic climber gold rush. Now, to mark the 50th anniversary of the incident, you can sport one of several new t-shirts or hats ($29.95-$44.95) memorializing this infamous moment in Yosemite climbing history. All proceeds support the Yosemite Climbing Association and the Yosemite Climbing Museum.

Have I used one? No. Do I desperately want one? Yes. As a 5’2” climber, I’m often just a few inches short of being able to make the clip from a good stance. I’ve heard wonderful things about the stiffy Kong Panic Draw ($54.95-$59.95) that lets you clip bolts otherwise out of reach. Maybe 2027 will be the year I finally gift myself one! If there’s a short or easily panicked sport climber in your life, I bet they would be stoked to get this as a present. Or you could make one for them DIY-style yourself.

The oversized, durable Metolius Climbers Tote ($64.95) has become my go-to gym bag. It easily fits my personal gear plus a 60m rope, or four sets of personal gear (shoes, harness, and chalkbag for me, my husband, and my two kids). Despite having stored weathered climbing shoes zippered inside for too long, this bag miraculously doesn’t stink yet either. It stows away nicely in gym cubbies (whereas my previous Cotopaxi tote would sort of spill out) and is easy to carry by hand or shoulder. This is a great mid-sized gift for the climber in your life, or you could fill it with other gear you’re gifting for a bigger impact.

Note on price: While this tote isn’t on sale at REI, co-op members get 20% off one full-price item through Nov. 30, so this is a good one to save on.

I receive a lot of climbing books and galleys to review and consider for coverage. Only some of them are so good that I can’t put them down. That’s how I felt about Erratica, a new collection of true stories from Moab-based, life-long climber Brian Laidlaw. Rare is the read that both keeps you attentive and entertained, yet also evokes deeper reflection. With Erratica, Brian manages to do just that. Pick up a copy ($20) at Moab’s Back of Beyond bookstore, or find one online anywhere books are sold.

Indian Actress and Director Nandita Das, Syrian Star Taim Hassan to Be Honored by Doha Film Festival

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Indian actress, director and social advocate Nandita Das and Syrian star Taim Hassan are set to be honored by the Doha Film Festival with Creative Excellence Awards during the event’s opening ceremony on Thursday.

Das has acted in more than 40 feature films, starting with Deepa Mehta’s “Fire,” which depicts a relationship between two women in an Indian household. She’s subsequently worked with top Indian directors such as Mrinal Sen, Shyam Benegal, Adoor Gopalakrishnan, Rituporno Ghosh and Mani Ratnam. Her directorial debut “Firaaq” — about the aftermath of the 2002 sectarian carnage in Gujarat, where 3,000 Muslims were killed — played at Toronto in 2008 and went on to travel widely and score many awards. She’s been a member of the main jury at Cannes Film Festival twice, in 2005 and 2013. Related Stories Netflix Orders YA Drama 'Prep' Based on Curtis Sittenfeld Novel From Nicole Taylor Russell Brand Documentary From 'Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story' Producers Set at Netflix Days After Rape Sentencing

Hassan made his breakthrough playing young ruler Fatimid Caliph Al-Adid in Syrian director Hatem Ali’s historical epics “Al-Zir Salem” (2000) and “Saladin” (2001). He has led all five seasons of hit Arabic TV show “Al Hayba,” about a family of arms smugglers working the border between Syria and Lebanon, a role that has made him a household name across the region and reached international audiences on Netflix. Hassan’s more recent work includes the show “Taj” (2024), in which he plays a boxer resisting the French occupation of Syria; hit political drama “Under the Seventh Ground” (2025); and thriller series “Mawlana” (2026).

“Cinema transcends borders, challenges perceptions and brings us closer to our shared humanity. Nandita Das and Taim Hassan represent the very best of this power,” said Doha Film Institute CEO Fatma Hassan Alremaihi, who also heads the festival. “Their remarkable artistry, courage of expression and unwavering commitment to authentic storytelling resonates far beyond geographical and cultural boundaries.”

As previously announced, Yemeni-Scottish filmmaker Sara Ishaq’s timely drama “The Station” — which looks at the impact of Yemen’s civil war on the country — is set to open the revamped event’s second edition, which will run Nov. 19-27 in the Qatari capital.
